TROY SCHOOL DISTRICT 
2012-13 PROPOSED  
GENERAL FUND BUDGET ASSUMPTIONS    
• The projected blended student count of 12,342.67 is an increase of 30.29 FTE’s from 2011/12. The 
February counts).   
• 2011/12 K-12 membership    12,312.38 
• 2012/13 K-12 membership    12,342.67 
• The foundation allowance of $8,805.00 per pupil for 2012/13 assumes a permanent $470.00 per 
pupil decrease. It also assumes additional, one time offsetting revenues of $116/pupil and 
$52/pupil for a net reduction of $302.00.  
• No budget reductions or revenue enhancements are included in the 2012/13 budget. 
Salary & Benefit Items 
• The retirement rate is budgeted with an increase from 24.46% to 27.37% on October 1, 2012.  The 
FICA rate of 7.65% has not changed. 
• MESSA Pak rates for employee benefits including life, disability, health, dental and vision are 
budgeted with a fixed cost to the district for single, 2 person, and family plans due to negotiated 
premium caps.  MESSA is the provider for members of the Troy Education Association (TEA) and 
Troy Educational Secretaries Association (TESA). 
• Employee benefit costs for health, dental, vision, life and disability for Troy Education Support 
Personnel Association (TESPA) are budgeted with a fixed cost to the district for single, 2 person, 
and family plans.  
• From the amended budget of 2011/12, salary expenditures have decreased $455,263 in the General 
Fund.  
There is a 0.0% salary schedule increase included in this budget for teachers.  There are also no 
step increases, but there are degree advancement increases included.  This budget also includes a 
net increase of 8.0 FTE’s (+7.0 full day Kindergarten, 1.0 science coordinator). 
There is a 0.00% salary increase for administrators included within this budget.  The budget 
includes an increase of two administrators in salary and benefits, and a reduction of two 
administrators in contracted staff.

pupil membership formula changed in 2011/12 from 75% / 25% to 90% / 10% (September/

DRAFT
There is a 2.00% salary decrease along with no step increases for TESA employees included within 
this budget.   
There is a 0.00% salary increase along with no step increases for TESPA employees included 
within this budget.   
There is a 0% salary increase included within this budget for non-represented employees. The 
budget also reflects no change in FTE. 
Other Non-salary Items 
• The General Fund is receiving transfers for indirect costs from the following funds: 
Community Education Fund $589,100 
Food Service Fund  $140,627 
Durant Fund   $    2,153 
Troy Career Center  $100,000 
Grants    $  39,775 
• The budget includes estimated grant revenues and expenditures for 2012/13.  The estimates are 
primarily based on the 2011/12 grant award amounts when 2012/13 grant awards are not known. 
The estimated grant budgets also include any salary or other increases noted above.  
• The General Fund estimated expenses exceed revenues by $3,178,288.  The projected fund balance 
at June 30, 2013 is $11,802,567.  The Durant Fund continues to have a fund balance of $5,610,166. 
Combined, the General Fund and Durant Fund will have an estimated total fund balance of 
$17,412,733. 
• Beginning with the 2010/11 school year, required accounting changes for Special Revenue Funds 
are reflected in the budget.  Athletics and Durant Funds are no longer eligible to be a special 
revenue fund and are included in the general fund.  General Fund revenues, expenditures and fund 
balance now include Athletics and Durant activities. 
• Beginning with the 2011/12 school year, the Troy Career Center grants have been moved to a 
Special Revenue Fund and will no longer be reported with General Fund revenues and 
expenditures. 
• Beginning with the 2012/13 school year, facility rental activities have been moved to a Special 
Revenue Fund and will no longer be reported with General Fund revenues and expenditures. 
• A new Capital Project Maintenance Fund was established for the 2011/12 school year.  The 
purpose is to have resources available to meet capital infrastructure repairs and preventative 
maintenance costs when they occur.  A preventative maintenance planning schedule is being 
developed to support the timing and priorities of this fund.

Fiscal Year 2012-13 Grants 
 
 
GENERAL FUND GRANTS 
 
Adult Education Civics ($20,000)
 
 – Federal funds to support projects that demonstrate 
effective and innovative practices in providing and increasing access to programs and services 
that integrate English literacy and civics education. 
Adult Education Family Literacy ($120,000)
 
 – Federal funds to meet the extemporaneous 
needs of the adult education faculty to upgrade their skills and competencies. 
Career Development Grant ($60,238)
 
 – Funds from Oakland Schools to embed career 
development in general curriculum delivery, counseling and other support activities and is 
consistent with Oakland Schools’ regional career development framework. 
IDEA-Flowthrough ($1,899,776)
 
 – Federal funds to provide the instructional services for 
students with learning disabilities, emotional impairments or other eligibility designations as 
outlined in the IEP. 
IDEA-Preschool ($62,777)
 
 – Federal funds to provide services for “at risk” 3 -6 year old 
preprimary impaired children and their parents. 
Michigan School Readiness ($108,800)
 
 – State funded preschool program to serve “at risk” 
four year old students and their parents. 
CTE Millage ($70,080)
 
 – County millage funds to support career and technical education 
programs. 
Title I ($802,614)
 
 –  Federal funds to help disadvantaged students meet high standards by 
providing instructional services to eligible K-8 students in reading, writing, science, math and 
social studies; provides parental involvement support; and instructional support for homeless 
students who attend the Troy School District. 
Title II, Part A, Teacher/Principal Training ($254,920)
 
 – Federal funds to implement 
smaller class sizes in grades K-3 and to implement professional development opportunities in 
the district to support building level, district level and curricular student achievement goals. 
Title III, English Language Acquisition Program ($528,160) – Federal funds to support 
English language learners from Pre-Kindergarten through 12th
 
 grade. 
 
 
TROY CAREER CENTER GRANTS 
 
Workforce Investment Act ($3,210,292) – Federal funds  to provide employers and youth 
and adult job seekers with seamless, integrated access to all workforce development 
programs and services operated by the Oakland County Michigan Works! Service Centers.

Mrs. Anne Mull, Director of Instruction and Assessment, 
presented a report to the Board on the recommendation 
grades  6‐12  began  the  process  of  clarifying  the 
knowledge  and  skills  that  students  would  need  to  be 
college  and  career  ready  in  social  studies.   Teachers 
examined  the  college  readiness  standards,  grade‐level 
Standards  in  literacy  relevant  to  social  studies.  During 
that  time,  teachers  had  the  opportunity  to  meet 
representatives  as  well  as  view  and  interact  with 
products  from  vendors.  All  materials  were  highly 
interactive and allowed students complete on‐line access 
to  the  text.  A  presentation  was  completed  in  February 
for  teachers  and  administrators,  which  resulted  in  the 
recommendations provided in Mrs. Mull’s report.   In her 
presentation, she reviewed the social studies curriculum 
that is currently being taught and future changes that will 
be implemented.  She informed the Board that Teachers’ 
Curriculum  Institute  (TCI)  was  chosen  for  Grade  6 
Regions  and  Grades  9  and  12  World  History.  Houghton 
Mifflin  Harcourt  was  chosen  for  Grades  7,  8  and  10  for 
their connection with the History Channel and interactive 
features  that  can  be  downloaded  to  multiple  devices.  
She  stated  that  it  was  time  for  this  textbook  adoption 
because more online resources and materials are needed 
to engage students in both school and at home. 
5.   She  stated  that  over  the  last  decade,  many  Troy 
School District teachers and administrators have worked 
to  ensure  that  the  District’s  English  language  arts 
curriculum and instructional practice have led students to 
the  highest  levels  of  academic  performance.   She 
indicated  that  the  implementation  of  the  new  Common 
Core  State  Standards  has  obligated  the  District  to 
evaluate  its  curriculum,  instructional  practices,  and 
materials. Past experience and ongoing research have led 
District staff to a renewed commitment to the workshop 
model  for  instruction  at  the  elementary  level.   In  an 
effort to maintain equity, access, and alignment vertically 
between grades and horizontally between buildings, our 
K‐12  curriculum  and  materials  have  been  under  review 
consistent  with  the  District’s  materials  adoption 
schedule. She also stated that this ongoing work resulted 
in  the  purchase  of  Houghton  Mifflin  Harcourt  (HMH) 
Literature for grades 9‐12 in July 2010, and grades 6‐8 in 
July 2011.   She added that after extensive review of the 
elementary  program,  the  committee  has  recommended 
the purchase of Houghton Mifflin Harcourt materials for

RECOGNITION 
Prudential Spirit of Community 
United States' largest youth recognition program based 
exclusively on volunteer community service. The program 
has honors middle level and high school students for 
outstanding service to others  at the local, state, and 
national level. This year, Sara Ma, a junior at Troy High 
School, was awarded a bronze metal and recognized as a 
Distinguished finalist for her work in promoting tolerance 
and diversity.  A representative  from Prudential Financial, 
Hitesh Parikh, shared some  remarks about Sara and 
presented here with the bronze medal award.   
The Board congratulated Sara for her achievement.

REPORT- Oakland 
Intermediate School District 
School District’s Budget.  Effective in 2004, Section 624 of 
the Revised School Code made it a requirement for every 
Intermediate School District to submit a copy of its budget 
to local school districts no later than May 1.  Local 
districts are required to pass a resolution either in support 
or disapproval of the budget no later than June 1.

Mr. Rajter gave a presentation on Oakland Intermediate 
Approve Oakland Intermediate 
WHEREAS: 
 1. Section 624 of the Revised School Code, as 
amended, requires the intermediate school board to 
submit its proposed budget no later than May 1 of 
each year to the board of each constituent district 
for review; and 
 2. Not later than June 1 of each year, the board of 
each constituent district shall review the proposed 
intermediate school district budget, shall adopt a 
board resolution expressing its support for or 
disapproval of the proposed intermediate school 
district budget, and shall submit to the 
intermediate school board any specific objections 
and proposed changes th e constituent district 
board has to the budget. 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED THAT: 
 1. The Troy Board of Education has received and 
reviewed the proposed intermediate school district 
budget in accordance with Section 624 of the 
Revised School Code, as amended, and, by the 
adoption of this resolution, expresses its support 
for the proposed intermediate school district 
budget.  The Troy School District Board of 
Education believes that it is the responsibility of the 
Intermediate School District to be responsible for 
its own operations, including adoption of its 
budget.  The Troy School District’s support of the 
Oakland Intermediate School District’s budget is for 
the sole purpose of complying with Section 624 of 
the Revised School Code. 
 2. The secretary of the Troy Board of Education or 
his/her designee shall forward a copy of this 
resolution to the intermed iate school board or its 
superintendent no later than June 1, 2012. 
 3. All resolutions insofar as they conflict with this 
resolution be and the same are hereby rescinded. 
Yes:        6 
No:         0 
Absent:   Underwood*  
*Ms. Underwood had to excuse herself early from the 
meeting then returned for the Workshop. 
Resolution declared adopted.

(NON-AGENDA ITEMS) 
OTHER 
Adoption of Oakland Schools 
WHEREAS, Public Act 419 of 2004 amended the election 
procedures for Intermediate School Districts that elect 
board members at a meeting of representatives from 
constituent district school boards; and 
WHEREAS, Public Act 419 of 2004 requires a constituent 
school board to designate its representative and identify 
the Intermediate School Board candidate the board 
supports by a resolution adop ted not earlier than twenty-
one (21) days prior to the date of the election; and 
WHEREAS, Public Act 419 of 2004 prescribes the method 
for passage of a resolution including the requirement to 
consider the resolution at no t less than one (1) public 
meeting before adopting the resolution; and 
WHEREAS, the Board considers this resolution at the open 
meeting conducted under the Open Meetings Act on 
Tuesday, May 15, 2012. 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED THAT: 
1. The Board designates Mr. Todd Miletti as its 
representative to serve on the 2012 electoral body 
responsible for electing members to the Oakland 
Schools Intermediate School District Board of 
Education. 
2. The Board supports candidate Mr. Mark Katz for 
one (1) position on the Oakland Schools

8. B.  PERSONNEL 
CONSIDERATION:  Administrative Appointment –  
      Assistant Principal, Athens High School 
I am pleased to recommend the appointment of Mr. William Turner to the 
position of Assistant Principal, Athens High School.  Mr. Turner came to 
Troy from Utica Community  Schools in June 2005. He served Utica 
Community Schools as an English teacher for three years and as a counselor 
for two years . Mr. Turner was hired by the Troy School District as a 
counselor for Troy High School in June of 200 5. In addition, Mr. Turner also 
became the principal of our High School Summer School program in 2009. 
Mr. Turner earned a Bachelor of Arts degree in 1998, a Masters degree in 
2003, and graduated from the Oakland Schools Leadership Aca demy in 
2008. With your approval, Mr. William Turner  will begin his duties as 
Assistant Principal, Athens High School effective July 1, 2012. 
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Acceptance of the resolution below: 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by         
 Supported by        
BE IT THEREFORE RESOLVED  that the Board of Education issues a two -year 
probationary administrative contract without tenure in the administrative position to 
Mr. William Turner , who will begin his duties as Assistant Principal, Athens High  
School, effective July 1, 2012.

A.  CONSIDERATION:  RENEWAL OF CONTRACTED SERVICES – 
TRANSPORTATION 
On April 21 , 2009, the Board approved a resolution allowing the 
Administration to enter into a contract with First Student to provide 
transportation services for the district . Attached is a resolution for 
Board consideration to extend the existing contract. 
 RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Accept the resolution below. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by:   
 Supported by: 
 Be it therefore resolved  that the Troy Board of Education authorize 
administration to proceed with finalizing  the contract renewal with First 
Student to provide transportation services for the 201 2/2013 and 
2013/2014 school years. 
 Yes: 
 No: 
311
Memorandum 
 DATE: June 14, 2012 
 TO: Barbara A. Fowler, Ph.D., Superintendent 
Business 
Services 
 FROM: Mark S. Rajter, Assistant Superintendent Business 
4400 Livernois 
 RE: First Student, Inc. Contract Extension Recommendation   
Troy, MI 48098 
248/823-4025,  
Fax - 248/823-4013 
I approached Doug Meek with First Student, Inc. (First Student) requesting  a proposal to renew 
their contract with T roy Schools. Their proposal is  a two year extension until June 2014. It calls 
for a cost of living rate adjustment (COLA) of 1.5% increase in fiscal 2013 and a 1% increase in 
fiscal 2014. Though the current operating agreement calls for increases to be that of CPI, First 
Student honored the district’s request that it could not  afford a 3.5% increase  (2012 CPI) while 
our overall student funding was decreasing for FY 2013.  
Furthermore, First Student has a three year contract with their drivers, represented by the MEA, 
for approximately a 1.8% wage increase for fiscal 2012, 2013 , and 2014. First Student was 
successfully able to get relief from their existing agreement with their bargaining unit 
representatives. 
First Student also agreed to remove any language regarding ownership of buses, allowing for 
the complete flee t to remain 100% owned by the school district. We also agreed to remove the 
limitation of an average fleet life of no more than eight (8) years. This will allow the district 
flexibility in replacing buses in the coming years. 
First Student has agreed to install digital cameras in all current and new buses purchased by the 
district. The costs of the cameras will solely be that of First Student. 
First Student realized reductions in their revenue because of reduced bus routes which the 
district implemented in years tw o and three of the agreement. Originally, they started with 70 
routes in 2010 ; we are now down to 57 routes in 2012. They accepted our request for route 
reductions to address the district financial challenges without holding us to the original bid 
specifications and contract agreement. They also allowed for the district to maintain ownership 
of its total fleet, whereby year three of the initial agreement called for them to begin replacing 24 
of our buses and transferring ownership of new buses to them. 
Based upon their performance of delivering satisfactory transportation needs , our positive 
working relationship and partnership, and their willingness to meet our challenges, we 
recommend to the Board that they accept this offer and direct the Superintendent,  or her 
designee, to successfully develop and authorize a contract to be executed with First Student for 
the period covering July 1, 2012 through June 30, 2014.

313
Memorandum 
 DATE: June 14, 2012 
 TO: Barbara A. Fowler, Ph.D., Superintendent 
Business 
Services 
 FROM: Mark S. Rajter, Assistant Superintendent Business 
4400 Livernois 
 RE: Enviro-Clean Contract Extension Recommendation   
Troy, MI 48098 
248/823-4025,  
Fax - 248/823-4013 
I originally approached Enviro-Clean to request a proposal of them to renew their contract  with 
Troy Schools. They responded very favorably by  offering to hold the current pricing and staffing 
levels for another two (2) years for both Custodial and Grounds services they provide Troy 
Schools.  
They have  offered to extend our current agreement with two (2) – one-year options , while 
maintaining their present pricing for both Custodial and Grounds positions through June of 2014. 
Their offer sheet elements will be incorporated into a formal contract amendment. 
The original contract was also a fixed three -year price for services. This offer will therefore hold 
our cost for these two areas of operation at the same cost level as of the 2009-10 fiscal years.  
Envrio-Clean consistently throughout this contract term have shown a willingness to respond to 
the District’s financial challenges. They agreed to reduced staffing numbers from the original 
agreement in both years two and three after administration approached them with the need for 
budget expenditure reductions. This effectively reduced their fees by hundreds of thousands of 
dollars. They have proven to be a partner with the District in not only meeti ng our custodial and 
grounds needs but also responding favorably to financial constraints.   
Based on the District’s positive working relationship and partnership with Enviro -Clean for the 
past three fiscal years, we recommend  to the Board that they accep t this offer and direct the 
Superintendent, or her designee, to successfully develop and authorize a contract to be 
executed with Enviro-Clean for the period covering July 1, 2012 through June 30, 2014.

On June 1, 2012 bids were opened for the District’s Beverage Vending Program.  Bids were sent 
to eleven (11) potential bidders.  Bids were received from two (2) vendors, none of which was a 
no bid.  All bid packets were reviewed by Mark Rajter, Assistant Superintendent, Gayle Moran, 
Sodexo General Manager, and myself.  We also conducted interviews with each of the vendors 
to gain a better understanding of their proposal and vending programs. 
We recommend approval for Dr. Fowler, or her designee, to develop and authorize a contract 
with Coca-Cola for an initial three (3) year contract with additional one (1) year renewals not to 
exceed two (2) additional years.  Coca-Cola has been the District’s Beverage Vending provider for 
the past 10 years.  Their proposal utilized conservative sales volumes and increased support for 
District programs.  The estimated revenue based on the initial three year contract is $117,545. 
Please include this as an action item on the June 19th Board Agenda.

344
STUDENT RIGHTS AND RESPONSIBILITIES     J‐2000 
J‐2013  SEXTING  
While  on  school  property  or  while  attending  school‐sponsored  or  school‐
related activities, whether on or off school property, sending, sharing viewing 
or  merely  possessing  sexually  explicit  digital  pictures,  messages,  text 
messages,  emails  or  other  material  of  a  sexual  nature  in  electronic  form  or 
other form on a computer, cell phone or other electronic device, is forbidden 
regardless of whether or not state and/or federal child pornography law has 
been violated. 
All  individuals  involved  in  sexting  are  hereby  placed  on  notice  of  the 
following:  
any  suspected  investigation  of  a  sexting  incident,  a  school  official  may 
search  a  student’s  cell  phone,  computer  or  other  electronic  device  if 
reasonable  suspicion  exists  that  a  student  has  been  involved  in  sexting.  
All evidence and electronic devices shall be turned‐over to the appropriate 
law enforcement agency, and will not be retained by the district. 
 Students  are  hereby  placed  on  notice  that  in  any  suspected  incident  of 
sexting the police and their parents/guardians may be contacted regarding 
the  incident  when  the  facts  so  warrant.   Additionally,  if  reasonable 
suspicion that sexting has occurred, the school shall report the suspected 
activity to the appropriate authorities related to suspected child abuse or 
neglect.  
 When students violate prohibitions of this policy, they shall be subject to 
disciplinary  action,  including  losing  the  privilege  of  bringing  the  device 
onto  school  property  and  being  reported  to  their  parent/guardian.  A 
violation  also  may  result  in  a  report  being  made  to  law  enforcement.  In 
addition, an administrator may confiscate the device, which shall only be 
returned  to  the  student’s  parent/guardian  or  surrendered  to  the  police  if 
reasonable suspicion exists. 
345
 Students and their parents/guardians are 
hereby placed on  notice that  in 
 Students shall comply with any additional rules developed by the school 
concerning  appropriate  use  of  telecommunication  or  other  electronic 
devices. 
 Students shall not utilize a telecommunication or similar electronic device 
in  a  manner  that  would  violate  the  District’s  Acceptable  Use  policy  or 
procedures or its Code of Acceptable Behavior and Discipline. 
Adopted:    
346
 TRADEMARKS, SERVICE MARKS AND TRADE NAMES     K-1600 
K-1601 TROY SCHOOLS TRADEMARKS, SERVICE MARKS, TRADE 
NAMES AND OTHER INTELLECTUAL PROPERTY 
 The Board of Education recognizes the importance of protecting its 
trademarks, service marks, intellectual property and trade names from 
unauthorized use.  It is, therefore, the policy of the Board to implement a 
trademark registration and licensing policy as follows: 
 All of the trademarks, service marks, trade name logos, symbols and/or 
any other intellectual property of Troy School District are registered 
trade/service marks with the Secretary of State, State of Michigan, or are 
entitled to protection under the common law.  It is the policy of the 
Board to actively defend the service trademark status.  Any use of a Troy 
School District’s trademark, service marks, trade name or any other 
intellectual property must have prior written approval of the 
Superintendent or designee and must be produced, manufactured or 
sold under authorization by Troy School District.   
Adopted:

     AGENDA FORMAT FOR REGULAR MEETINGS     B‐1300 
  1.  The agenda for meetings of the Board is prepared by the 
superintendent and the Board president. 
  2.  The agenda for regular meetings is delivered to members five 
calendar days preceding the meeting. 
be posted at the Administrative Center four calendar days 
preceding the meeting.  The Board regular meeting agenda 
and board packet of supporting material will be made 
available on the district’s website the day of, but prior to, the 
Board meeting. 
  4.  The agenda for special meetings is delivered to members at 
least twenty‐four hours before the meeting. 
  5.  To add/delete an item to/from the agenda, a member proposes 
the addition/deletion by a motion which must be seconded 
and approved by a vote of at least four members.

   E‐MAIL RETENTION AND GUIDELINES    C‐1500 
C‐1501  E‐MAIL RETENTION 
This Policy applies to all Users, except student users, of any District E‐mail 
system whether owned or operated by a third party on behalf of the Board, 
school or unit. For purposes of this policy, User shall mean employees, officers, 
temporary employees, interns, vendors, consultants, contractors, and 
authorized agents and volunteers who use the District E‐Mail system(s).  
A. Mailbox Size and Management   
1. The District E‐Mail system(s) are to be used as a method of communication 
among employees and other authorized users for business purposes. It is 
not intended to be a medium in which to permanently store electronic 
information. Where the Technology Resource Center (“TRC”) has mailbox 
size or attachment restrictions in place, Users are required to manage the 
space in their mailbox appropriately. 
2. The retention periods described in this Policy do not guarantee that a User’s 
mailbox size will accommodate the storage of all E‐Mail messages for the 
duration of the retention period. Therefore, Users should regularly review 
their messages and take timely action on long term retention needs. To stay 
within mailbox size limitations, E‐Mail messages with large attachments 
should be saved to offline storage or printed immediately and then deleted 
from the mailbox.  
B. Retention Period   
1. E‐Mail message and Note items more than one (1) year in age will be  
automatically deleted by TRC from a User’s mailbox. Any E‐Mail messages 
and Note items, including document attachments that need to be retained 
for longer than one (1) year either (a) pursuant to law, (b) an order of a 
court, (c) at the direction of the Superintendent, (d) pursuant to the Board’s 
records policy (K‐1002), (e) pursuant to the Records Retention and Disposal 
Schedule of Michigan Public Schools (Educational Bulletin #522) or that 
have administrative or historical value must be printed out or copied to 
offline or local storage by the User.   All backups of the District Email  
350
E‐MAIL RETENTION AND GUIDELINES    C‐1500 
C‐1501  E‐MAIL RETENTION (Cont.) 
system will only be kept for (2 business days) to ensure system restoration 
in the event of a technical issue or disaster recovery need.   TRC shall 
implement a system to suspend the routine for destroying E‐Mails outlined 
herein when requested as part of a litigation hold by the Superintendent’s 
Office. This suspension will be in place until TRC is directed otherwise by 
the Superintendent’s Office.    
2. Electronic Calendars and Task items located on the E ‐ mail system will be   
available for a period of two years to comply with the Records Retention 
and Disposal Schedule for Michigan Public Schools.  
C.  E‐Mail Archive  
1.  E‐Mail messages may be public records and therefore, beginning July 1,  
2012, the District E‐Mail system will utilize an archive system that will 
automatically record and retain User E‐Mail messages for a period of one 
year. TRC will automatically destroy archived E‐Mail messages more than 
one (1) year in age in accordance with this Policy. This retention period and 
purge process will be applied automatically to a User’s archived E‐Mails 
except when a User’s E‐Mail messages and resources have been 
quarantined for litigation purposes.  
D. School‐Based E‐Mail Systems  
1.    Any school which elects to operate its own E‐Mail system must ensure that  
its system complies with the requirements of this Policy and all other 
applicable Board Policies, guidelines and standards as a condition for 
continued operation.    
Adopted: 
351
   GENERAL SECTION      G-1000 
G-1006 PLACEMENT OF TEACHERS 
 The Superintendent or designee shall determine teacher placement based on 
qualifications (as defined by the District, which shall include but not be 
limited to state and federal requirements such as certification, Highly 
Qualified requirements, endorsements, etc.), the academic needs and best 
interest of District students, and the District’s educational program.  Teacher 
preference(s) may also be considered.  At all times, the District shall strive to 
place the most effective and qualified teachers in assignments aligned with 
student and District needs. 
For purposes of this policy, “placement” shall mean and include the  
filling of vacancies, voluntary and involuntary transfers, job sharing, 
and any decision which results in the placement of a teacher in an assignment 
deemed by the District to be a teaching assignment; “vacancy” shall mean 
and include any unoccupied position to be filled by the Board, after all other 
positions have been filled by District assignments, transfers, or recalls, in the 
manner and to the extent determined by the District as appropriate.   
For purposes of this policy, “teacher” includes individuals whose 
employment is regulated by the Tenure Act, including certain school 
administrators (individuals with teaching certificates as defined by the 
Teacher Certification Code), who are assigned to positions within the District 
for which the Michigan Department of Education (MDE) requires a teaching 
certificate.  Individuals who do not possess teaching certificates but are 
serving a probationary period under the Tenure Act are also considered a 
“teacher” for purposes of this policy.  Individuals who may possess a 
teaching certificate, but are assigned to a position for which a certificate is not 
required, are not subject to this policy or its implementing regulations (e.g., 
school social workers, school psychologists, school nurses, occupational 
therapists, etc). 
Teacher placement decisions, and the impact of such decisions on the  
individual teacher or the bargaining unit, shall not be the subject of any 
352
terms or conditions within a collective bargaining agreement between the 
District and a collective bargaining representative of such teachers.  
 The Superintendent or designee shall develop and adopt administrative  
 guidelines/procedures/rules/regulations related to teacher placement.    
Adopted:  
353
   STAFF COMMUNITY RELATIONS     G‐1400 
G‐1401  POLITICAL ACTIVITIES 
  The Board recognizes the right of employees to campaign for and to 
hold elected public office. 
  Candidates may not campaign during school hours, use school district 
supplies or equipment for political campaigning, or involve students or 
candidates or election issues. 
  No  material  supporting  any  candidate,  candidates,  slate  of  candidates, 
political issues, issues which cause substantial and material interference 
and/or  disruption  with  normal  school  activities,  or  political 
organizations/committees may be distributed, posted, or displayed on or 
within any district‐owned property. The display of material on personal 
vehicles  while  parked  on  school  property  during  school  hours  is 
permitted,  provided  it  does  not  cause  substantial  or  material 
interference and/or disruption with normal school activities.   
All  school  personnel,  including  contracted  personnel  assigned  to  the 
school  district,  shall  maintain  a  posture  of  complete  neutrality  with 
respect  to  all  candidate(s),  political  issues,  and/or  political 
organizations/committees  while  engaged  in  duties  for  the  Troy  School 
District or in contact with students or students’ parents. 
Further,  absent  the  express  written  consent  of  the  superintendent  or 
his/her  designee,  all  school  personnel  shall  be  restricted  from  utilizing 
Troy School District resources, technology, including District e‐mail, and 
student/parent contact information received while engaged in duties for 
the  Troy  School  District  for  the  purpose  of  supporting  any  candidate, 
candidates,  slate  of  candidates,  political  issues,  including  any  issues 
which  cause  substantial  and  material  interference  and/or  disruption 
with normal school activities, or political organizations/committees. 
 This policy does not apply to any candidate, candidates, slate of 
candidates, community or political groups who have reserved/rented 
354
other school personnel in their campaigns during any school
 day. 
  Also, employees may not do any of the above on behalf
 of other 
time in a building or room for meeting purposes or in connection 
with a class project or activities.

SAFETY AND SUPERVISION OF STUDENTS        J‐3200 
J‐3203  CHILDREN’S INTERNET PROTECTION ACT POLICY 
  It is the intention of the Troy School District that its Internet safety 
policies and technology protection measures comply with the provisions 
of the Children’s Internet Protection Act (CIPA), 47 USC 254(h) as 
amended and Protecting Children in the 21st Century Act, Pub. L. No. 
110-385, Title II, 122 Stat. 4096 (2008).  Accordingly the Administration is 
directed to take all actions necessary and appropriate to implement and 
enforce CIPA and Protecting Children in the 21st Century Act mandates 
through administrative regulations and building policies with respect to 
student access to and use of the Internet through the District’s computer 
network.  Such administrative regulations implementing the CIPA and 
Protecting Children in the 21st Century Act mandates shall cover the 
following areas regarding students’ utilization of the District’s electronic 
network: 
  1.  Access by students to inappropriate matter on the Internet 
and the World Wide Web; 
  2.  The safety and security of students when using electronic 
mail, chat rooms, and other forms of direct electronic 
communications; 
other unlawful activities by students online; 
  4.  Unauthorized disclosure, use, and dissemination of personal 
identification information regarding students; and
  5.  Measures designed to restrict students’ access to materials 
harmful to minors.; and 
             6.   Provide for the education of minors about appropriate online 
behavior, including interacting with other individuals on 
social networking sites and in chat rooms, and cyber‐
bullying awareness and response. 
356
  3.  Unauthorized access, 
including so‐called “hacking,” and
